# Frontend overview

What the Angular app contains today and the details a change must keep. The rules are in `.claude/rules/frontend.md`, `testing.md` and `testing-frontend.md`; the HTTP shapes in `docs/api-contract.md`.

## Shell, routing, auth

- `src/index.html`: title, meta description, `<link rel="canonical">` and Open Graph tags (`og:url`, `og:image` `https://nfnn.cz/images/og-image.jpg` 1200×630 JPEG, a placeholder cut from the hero until real content arrives, `twitter:card`); `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (`CanonicalLink.start()` from an app initializer, `SITE_ORIGIN` `https://nfnn.cz`) rewrites the canonical link and `og:url` to the path of every navigation without query or fragment; `public/robots.txt` keeps crawlers out of `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/darce/`, `/sprava/` and the link pages and names `public/sitemap.xml` (home and the privacy policy; news and project pages wait for a generated sitemap); icons (`favicon.svg` is the only linked tab icon, everything that can be SVG on the site is SVG, Jakub 2026-10-30; `favicon.ico`, linked from nowhere, answers services that ask only the default address `/favicon.ico`: without it claude.ai showed the DuckDNS duck for the connector, the icon of the parent domain; `apple-touch-icon.png`, which iOS takes only as PNG; `app-icon.svg`, linked from nowhere, is the icon for the ZimaOS app list, set there as its icon URL since 2026-10-30); loads nothing from other sites. Launch (#348): `<app-root>` holds a static launch screen (`.launch-screen`, `src/styles/_launch-screen.scss`: the theme's `icon-512.png` at `--launch-icon-size` in the middle of `--color-surface`), which the page paints before any script runs and Angular replaces with the first page; `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` (with `mobile-web-app-capable`, which keeps Chrome from warning) makes iOS fetch the `apple-touch-startup-image` links when the site is added to the Home Screen (iOS 27 fetched none without it), one per iPhone and iPad screen and orientation of `STARTUP_SCREENS` in `scripts/make-startup-images.mjs`, which the `postbuild` of `npm run build` draws into `startup/<width>x<height>.png` of the build from the built manifest's `background_color`, `icon-512.png` and the built stylesheet's `--launch-icon-size` (no library: it reads and writes the PNGs itself), so the launch image iOS shows at once equals the page's first paint and a theme brings its own; `scripts/make-startup-images.test.mjs` keeps the links of `index.html` equal to that list. The images are not in `ngsw-config.json`: iOS keeps its own copy from the moment of adding, so an installed app shows a changed launch image only after it is added again. `main.ts` bootstraps and references `@angular/localize`. `styles.scss` loads the theme, reset, keyframes; the scrollbars are the browser's own (Jakub 2026-11-05: no custom scrollbar styles).
- Zoom (Jakub 2026-10-31, plan v3 2.23d): the viewport meta in `src/index.html` is `width=device-width, initial-scale=1, maximum-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover` (Android Chrome and the desktop emulators then never pinch-zoom the page), `html { touch-action: manipulation }` in `styles.scss` stops double-tap zoom, and `core/zoom/page-pinch-lock.ts` (`PagePinchLock.start()` from the app initializer `pagePinchLockAtStart`) cancels Safari's `gesturestart`, `gesturechange` and `gestureend` on the document (non-passive), because Safari on the iPhone and iPad ignores the viewport limit; no `touchmove` listener, so scrolling never waits on script. The text size still follows the browser and the system (rem tokens); in the app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad, which has no aA menu, `core/zoom/system-text-size.ts` (`SystemTextSize.start()` from the app initializer `systemTextSizeAtStart`) puts `follows-system-text-size` on `html` when `navigator.standalone` is `true` on a touch screen (a Mac's Dock app has none), and `styles.scss` gives that root `font: -apple-system-body` with `font-family: var(--font-sans)`, so the root size is the iPhone's text size setting (Dynamic Type, 17 px at the default; Jakub 2026-11-05, Q13); Safari in a tab and every other browser keep 16 px and their own text size. Playwright's WebKit has no Dynamic Type, so the size itself is a device check. Photos and the Přispět QR open in `shared/ui/zoom-viewer`. At the largest system text (53 px, AX5) the fluid heading tokens shrink to what the phone's width allows (`max(1rem, min(<rem>, 16vw))` as their minimum, and `--text-hero` `max(1rem, min(3.25rem, 27vw))`, so a long Czech word and the hero's "naděje" still fit a phone's line; unchanged at the default size from 320 px and at 200 % from 390 px), `h1`–`h3` break a word only when it cannot fit (`overflow-wrap: break-word`, in `styles.scss`), the sign-in dialog's Přihlášení / Registrace switch stacks its buttons (`flex-wrap`), and a dialog's × keeps the size of its fixed button (`--text-dialog-close`); `e2e/largest-text.spec.ts` checks the header, the headings and the switch at 53 px on 320 and 375 px.
- Touch (#380, #385, 2026-11-06): a touch answers as in a native iOS app. `src/styles/_controls.scss` (loaded by `styles.scss` before the `ui-` blocks) removes Safari's grey tap flash (`-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on `html`) and makes every control (`a[href]`, `button`, `summary`, `[role="button"]`, `[role="tab"]`) unselectable and undraggable, without the long-press callout (no link preview with "Otevřít / Přidat do seznamu četby", no "Zkopírovat / Vyhledat" over a label), and decorative images (`img[alt=""]`) and images inside controls without callout and drag; links to another site, `mailto:` and `tel:` keep the callout, so the address can still be copied or shared, and links without a class inside running text (`p` other than the crumbs, `dd`, `address`, `app-markdown`) stay selectable with the system menu, as all content text, the bank details and every field do. A pressed state on `:active` (`src/styles/_pressed.scss`: `when-pressed` for an enabled control, `look`, which under `forced-colors` becomes a 2 px `Highlight` outline inside the control): by default a tint of the control's own text colour (`--color-pressed`, 8 % in `nova-nadeje`) laid as a background image over its background, with a weight (`:active:active` in `_controls.scss`) that wins over a component's base rule but loses to its own `:active` rule and to the `ui-` blocks; the solid `.ui-button` and `.ui-cta` darken (`--pressed-brightness`), the header's logo fades as a bar button of iOS does (`fades`: `--pressed-opacity`, 0.35 in `nova-nadeje`, at once on touch and back in `--duration-base`, with no tint, which drew a box around it), `.ui-cta--ghost` and the rows of `.ui-list` tint, a card that its link covers (news, featured news, project) shrinks (`--pressed-scale` in `--duration-fast`, back in its `--duration-base`; under `prefers-reduced-motion` it darkens instead) and a tab of the tab bar shows the pill (`--color-tab-pill`, whose label contrast `check-themes.mjs` already proves). Safari on the iPhone applies `:active` only while the document listens to touches, so `core/touch/pressed-on-touch.ts` (`PressedOnTouch.start()` from the app initializer `pressedOnTouchAtStart`) keeps a passive no-op `touchstart` listener on the document. Hover looks stand only inside `@media (hover: hover)`, so a tap leaves none behind (a card no longer stays lifted); the stylelint rule `nfnn/hover-only-with-hover` (`scripts/stylelint-hover-media.mjs`, run by `npm run lint:styles`, tested by `stylelint-hover-media.test.mjs`) refuses a `:hover` outside it. `e2e/touch-controls.spec.ts` checks what selecting a page takes and leaves; the callout and the pressed look are device checks, since Playwright's browsers know no `-webkit-touch-callout`.
- On an iPhone Safari 26 lets the page show under its status bar (Jakub 2026-11-05, replacing the blue strip of 2026-10-30). Safari 26 ignores `<meta name="theme-color">`, and the site sets none: WebKit (`LocalFrameView::fixedContainerEdges`) hit-tests the middle of the top edge 4 px inside the viewport, ignoring `pointer-events`, and walks up to the first fixed or sticky box at least 90 % as wide as the viewport (it skips one with `visibility: hidden`, opacity under 0.1, or 10 px or thinner). Without such a box, or when that box or anything on the way to it has a `backdrop-filter` (`PredominantColorType::Multiple`), it leaves the bar untinted with the page under it; otherwise it tints the bar with the first plain `background-color` on the way, and a fixed box without one is sampled once from the pixels under it and kept. The app header is `position: absolute` and the tab bar sits at the bottom, so no page has such a box at the top edge; a new fixed or sticky bar at the top keeps it that way with a `backdrop-filter` or changes the status bar with it. Chrome on Android shows its default toolbar. The app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad asks for the same with `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style" content="black-translucent">` (Jakub 2026-11-06: the landing photo runs under the clock); without it iOS starts the web view under the status bar and paints the bar in the page's background. Two things follow there, both measured in the iOS 27.2 simulator and set only under `@media (display-mode: standalone)` with `@supports (-webkit-touch-callout: none)` in `styles.scss`, so a Safari tab, Android and a Mac's Dock app keep `env(safe-area-inset-top)` and `100svh`. WebKit gives `100svh`, `100%` and the root's `clientHeight` the screen less the status bar, and when the document is no taller than that it shrinks the whole viewport to it (`innerHeight` 894 of 956 on an iPhone 18 Pro Max), so the fixed tab bar of the landing page floated a status bar's height above the bottom over an empty band; there `--full-screen-height` (`100svh` in the theme) becomes `calc(100svh + env(safe-area-inset-top))` and `body` takes it as its `min-height`, so every page is at least as tall as what the app shows and the viewport keeps the whole screen. Not `100lvh` (#552 until 2026-11-12): iOS keeps the status bar mode an app was added with, so an app added before the page asked for `black-translucent` still shows a solid status bar, and there the viewport is 894 of 956 while `100lvh` stays 956, so the landing page scrolled by the status bar's height (Jakub's iPhone); `100svh` plus the top inset is 956 in the new mode (894 + 62) and 894 in the old one (894 + 0), measured on an iPhone 18 Pro Max in the iOS 27.2 simulator. Playwright has neither mode, so `e2e/first-screen.spec.ts` resolves the rule's expression with the two measured sets of viewport units and checks that it gives exactly the visible height in both. And Safari blurs the top of the page under and just below the status bar (the scroll edge effect of iOS), which blurred the logo; there `--safe-area-top` (`env(safe-area-inset-top)` in the theme) adds `--home-screen-top-clearance` (16 px in `nova-nadeje`: the blur reaches about 25 pt below the status bar, measured with lines of text every 10 pt on an iPhone 18 Pro Max, and the header row centres the logo 14 px further down), and everything placed from the top safe area takes it: the header, the back button and its scroll edge, `scroll-padding-top`, the hero, the page intros, the first sections of O nás and Přispět, `.ui-panel-page`, the 404 page, the donor area, the administration, the side panel and a full-screen dialog with its ×. iOS may keep the status bar mode of an app added before, until it is added again; the clearance keeps the content out of the blur in either mode. Playwright can neither open a home-screen app nor emulate `display-mode`, so `e2e/status-bar.spec.ts` checks only the meta, and the rest is a device check (the photo under the clock, the tab bar at the bottom of the landing page and of a short page, a sharp logo). `e2e/status-bar.spec.ts` checks the top edge at 390 px on `/`, `/projekty` and the privacy policy (opened and scrolled) and on the donor overview, and that the page paints no tint and names no theme colour. `app.ts` renders `<router-outlet />` and the sign-in dialog while `LoginDialogService.isOpen()` (Back closes it, and goes back a step first from the code from the app and from the recovery by payment, through `DialogHistory`; a sign-in with `returnTo` hands its entry over to that page, the privacy policy link in the registration replaces it) (re-created on every open so panels start clean, except a half-filled registration that `LoginDialogService` keeps without its password until a sign-in or a sent registration; its "Přihlášení / Registrace" switch carries `aria-pressed` and shows only on those two starting steps). `app.config.ts`: `provideRouter` with `chunkLoadRecoveryFeature` (`withNavigationErrorHandler` handing every navigation error to `ChunkLoadRecovery`, below) and `withInMemoryScrolling` (anchor scrolling for `routerLink="/o-nas" fragment="o-nas"` links; the sections carry Czech ids without diacritics: `o-nas` `o-nas`, `kontakt` `aktuality`, `/o-nas`, `kontakt`), `provideHttpClient(withXhr(), withInterceptors([authInterceptor]))` (`appHttpClient`; XMLHttpRequest because the fetch backend reports no upload progress, which the backup upload of the first setup shows), session restore on start.
- `core/reload/` and `core/navigation/`: `reload-guard.ts` (`ReloadGuard.claim(reason)`, `ReloadReason` `'chunk-load'`, `'update'`, `'unrecoverable'`): one automatic reload per reason and 60 s (`RELOAD_WINDOW_MS`), the time kept in `sessionStorage` under `nfnn.reload.<reason>` (reached through the `SESSION_STORAGE` token); when the storage cannot be read, written or read back it allows none, so a tab can never reload in a loop. `chunk-load-recovery.ts`: `isChunkLoadError(error)` recognises a failed dynamic import by the browsers' messages (Chromium and Firefox "dynamically imported module", Safari "Importing a module script failed", the MIME-type refusals); `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` sets `failure` (`{ url, initial }`, `initial` while the router has shown no page yet, `Router.navigated`), kept until the next successful navigation, and loads the page that was asked for in the newest build (`AppUpdates.loadNewVersion` of the navigation's URL, which asks the service worker for the new build first) only when the browser is online (`Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()`, `navigator.onLine`), `ChunkProbe` says the reload would land and the guard allows it; `failure` is set before that load, so a browser that stays on the page (a cancelled unload) still shows the offer; `reload()` is the visitor's own reload of it. `chunk-probe.ts`: `ChunkProbe.reloadWouldLand(error)` asks the server with `fetch(…, { method: 'HEAD', cache: 'no-store', signal })` for the chunk the error names (a same-origin `.js`/`.mjs` address in the message), aborted after 4 s (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), and answers yes, when nothing redirected the request, for a 404 or an HTML page at the chunk's own address (a deploy), or, when the message names none (Safari), for an HTML answer of `/`; another answer, a redirect, a rejected or aborted request (the network is gone or hangs while `navigator.onLine` still says online) is no, so the recovery only offers the reload. `handleNavigationError` sets `failure` before it asks, counts the router's `NavigationStart` events and ignores an answer that comes after another navigation started. It sees only router navigations: a failed `import()` outside the router, a `@defer` block or a worker gets `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleImportFailure` in 1.9, which offers a reload and never reloads by itself (Phase 0 gate, J-m1). `ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer(url)` pushes the address as a step of the site's own history (`history.pushState`, which `own-history.ts` turns into a replace of the browser's entry) and reloads, or, for the same address or only another fragment (`/#prispet` on `/`), replaces it with `history.replaceState` and reloads, so a full load inside the site never adds a browser entry. `own-history.ts` (`installOwnHistoryIn(window)`, run by the side-effect import `install-own-history.ts` in `main.ts` before Angular starts, as the Trusted Types policy is; issue #431, Jakub 2026-11-06): the site keeps its own history on every device, so the browser holds one entry of the site and its Back (the Safari toolbar and swipe, Android's system back, the mouse's back button, Alt+←, ⌘[ on an iPad keyboard, VoiceOver's scrub) leaves the site or closes the installed app, and Safari has no page of the site to slide back under the finger. It patches `pushState`, `replaceState`, `back`, `forward` and `go` on `window.history`: a push replaces the browser's entry and is kept on the site's own stack (at most `OWN_HISTORY_LIMIT`, 50, in `sessionStorage` under `nfnn.app-history`, a name never renamed); `back()` and `go(-n)` restore the address and state of the step before with `replaceState` and then dispatch a real `PopStateEvent` on `window`, asynchronously as a browser does, so Angular's router (`navigationTrigger: 'popstate'`, `restoredState`), the scroll restoration of `withInMemoryScrolling`, `DialogHistory`, `BackTarget` and the public shell's `returningInHistory` work unchanged; `forward()` and `go(+n)` do nothing, as a native app has no forward, and `go(0)` reloads. A reload on the address the kept stack ends at (an update, a receipt restart) keeps the stack; another address starts afresh; storage that cannot be read or written leaves the stack in memory, and if the layer were missing the site would simply use the browser's history as before. Safari's limit of 100 `pushState`/`replaceState` calls per 10 s is unchanged, as each push becomes one replace. The site's own steps back come only from its controls: the header's Zpět, a dialog's × and, in the app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad, the swipe of `edge-swipe-back.ts`. Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/connectivity/` (plan v3 2.7): `Connectivity` (root, started by `connectivityAtStart` in `app.config.ts`) decides from what the API answers whether the app is offline; the one exception is the browser saying it is offline (`navigator.onLine` false at the start or its `offline` event), which is trusted at once, because a phone in airplane mode leaves requests hanging instead of failing them (Jakub 2026-11-06), and the probe that starts with it brings the app back online if the server answers; `navigator.onLine` true says nothing. `connectivityInterceptor` (the last interceptor) reports every `/api` outcome: an answer of the server (any status, or a gateway status with an `application/problem+json` body) counts as reached, while status 0 and 502, 503, 504 or 520–530 without a problem body (`isUnreachable`) count as failed; after a failure it probes at once and goes offline after 3 s (`OFFLINE_AFTER_MS`) without a success, then probes every 30 s (`PROBE_EVERY_MS`) while the tab is visible, at the window's `online` event and when the tab becomes visible again; the window's `offline` event and an answer the service worker served from its cache (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`, with a `Date` more than 15 s older than the server's clock, which it learns from network answers) only start a probe; a fresher one counts as contact while the app is online, but once it is offline it too only starts a probe, since a copy kept seconds ago reached no server. The probe (`connectivity-probe-api.ts`) is `GET /api/operations/setup` without the token, so it bypasses the service worker, with a 5 s timeout (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), after which the app is offline at once (cause `network`), without the 3 s wait a failed request starts; a probe's 5 s or a failure's 3 s that run out more than 1 s late (`SLEEP_TOLERANCE_MS`, measured with `Date.now()`) mean the device slept meanwhile (a locked iPhone or the app in the background freezes its timers, which then fire at once on waking), so the app asks the server once more instead of going offline (Jakub 2026-11-12: the label showed "Offline" after the phone woke up while the server answered in milliseconds); `wentOffline` emits each time the app goes offline. `cause` is `network` for status 0, a 504 of the worker or a browser that says offline, else `server`; `tryAgain()` probes on request. `kept-answer-refresh.ts` (`keptAnswerRefreshInterceptor`, before `serviceWorkerBypassInterceptor`): when the worker answers the home content, a news or project list or page with a copy whose `Date` is more than 15 s older than the server's clock and the app is not offline, the page gets the copy at once and the same request goes once more past the worker (`ngsw-bypass`), and its answer reaches the page as a second value only when its body differs; a failed or refused second request leaves the copy and is no error, and a request sent with `ngsw-bypass` counts as a network answer (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`). So a page that reads these answers takes every value it gets (`ContentDetail`), and one that needs a single answer takes the first (`NewsPage`'s year's projects, `take(1)`). `shown-answers.ts` (`ShownAnswers`) keeps the `Date` of each public answer the current page shows (reset at a navigation to another path), whose oldest is the time in the label; `data-time.ts` writes it as `9:30` today, `4. 11. 9:30` yesterday, else a day (`15. 3. 2026`), without leading zeros except the minutes (Jakub 2026-11-06). `unseen-photos.ts` (`UnseenPhotos`, started with it) marks an `<img>` that fails to load `is-unavailable` (the global style makes it transparent, so its box and its text stay and no broken-image sign shows) and takes the mark off when it loads; offline that is a photo the service worker never kept, since it keeps only photos already seen, in the size the screen asked for, up to 300 (Jakub 2026-11-05, Q8). `browserSaysOffline()` replaces the former `core/navigation/connectivity.ts` for the error reporter and the chunk-load recovery.
- `core/device/crypto/` (plan v3, 2.13; called by the store of 2.14, and by the administration copy from 2.19): `copy-keys.ts` (`OpenCopy.create()`, `sealRecord`/`openRecord` at a `RecordAddress`, `wrapWithPassword`/`wrapWithPrf`/`wrapWithSessionKey`, `rekeyed()`, which closes the old copy at once, so a caller reads every record first, re-keys, then seals and writes them all again, outside one IndexedDB transaction, which would commit across the WebCrypto awaits; `close()`; `openWithPassword`, `openWithPrf`, `openWithSessionKey`, `generateSessionKey`; refusals as `CopyRefused` `wrong-secret`, `wrong-wrap`, `damaged`, `closed`), `password-key.ts` (`deriveWithMeasuredParameters`: Argon2id 128 MiB when the worker timed it within 1 s, else 64 MiB; `WorkerPasswordKeyDerivation` starts `argon2.worker.ts` per derivation, 30 s timeout; the messages in `password-key-protocol.ts`, which the worker imports), `argon2.ts` (hash-wasm, only for the worker and tests), `prf.ts` (`prfInput` from the device salt; `readPrf` over the device's passkeys or any of the site, giving the passkey, its user handle and the output, which the caller zeroes), `device-registry.ts` (`lookupTag`, `userHandleTag`, `registryEntry`, no e-mail in the clear), `hkdf.ts`, `device-bytes.ts` (`framed` length-prefixed fields for AAD and HKDF info). How it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies".
- `core/version/` (plan v3 2.3): `api-generation.ts` (`API_GENERATION`, the server API generation the build speaks, 3), kept equal to `api-generation.json` by its spec, which the backend's `ApiGenerationTest` keeps equal to `VersionHeadersFilter.API_GENERATION`; the same files hold `POSTS_ACROSS_GENERATIONS` (the session refresh, the sign-out and the receipt status question), which both the interceptor and the filter let through, kept equal the same way; `server-version.ts` (`ServerVersion.observe(headers)` keeps the latest `Nfnn-Api` that is a whole number from 1 to 999 999 999 and the latest `Nfnn-Revision` the server would accept from a report (`revisionOf`, or `dev`), ignores anything else; signals `revision`, `apiGeneration`, `mustUpdate`, true while the latest generation differs from `APP_API_GENERATION`, a token that defaults to `API_GENERATION`); `version-interceptor.ts` (`versionInterceptor`, after `authInterceptor` in `app.config.ts`, so it also sees the refresh and the retried call): on every `/api/` call it sets `Nfnn-App: <APP_REVISION>` and `Nfnn-App-Api: <APP_API_GENERATION>` and reads the answer's headers, a refusal's too (not a status 0), which `ServerVersion` takes only from an answer whose `Cache-Control` says `no-store` (a cached answer may carry an old server's headers); while `mustUpdate`, a `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` other than `/api/donors/auth/refresh`, `/api/donors/auth/logout` and `/api/donations/receipts/status` is not sent and fails with an `HttpErrorResponse` 426 whose body is the problem `https://nfnn.cz/problems/app/update-required` (`UPDATE_REQUIRED`), title "Web mezitím dostal novou verzi", detail "Nic jsme neodeslali. Načtěte stránku znovu a zkuste to ještě jednou." (`layout.update.problem.*`). Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/security/trusted-types.ts` (plan v3, 2.5): `installScriptUrlPolicy(trustedTypes, document.baseURI)`, run by `install-trusted-types.ts`, the first import of `main.ts` (so before any other module of the app is evaluated), creates the Trusted Types policy `default` with only `createScriptURL`: `admittedScriptUrl(input, sink, base)` gives the worker constructor (`WORKER_SINK`) an address of the site's own origin, without user name, query or fragment, whose path is `/worker-<8 characters>.js` (the receipt PDF and QR reader workers as the build names them), the service worker registration (`SERVICE_WORKER_SINK`) only `/ngsw-worker.js`, each resolved, and `null` (refused) for any other address or sink; without `trustedTypes`, with a default policy already there or when the browser refuses the policy, nothing is installed and the app starts anyway. The Content-Security-Policy that requires it is in `nginx-security-headers.conf` (`docs/security-overview.md`, Browser and transport).
- `core/live/` (plan v3 2.16): `live-api.ts` asks `GET /api/live` as a text stream (`observe: 'events'`, `reportProgress`), `event-stream.ts` (`EventStreamReader`) cuts the growing text into events (`event:` and `data:` lines, comments skipped), and `live-updates.ts` (`LiveUpdates`, root) keeps one stream while a donor is signed in, a page listens (`changes(feeds)`, which emits on a hint of one of those feeds and once after a reopening that follows a stream lost while it listened, since hints in between are lost; a page that started listening while the stream waited for a retry is told too, since hints of the wait are lost to it, while a page whose arrival opened the stream again has just read everything and is not told) and the tab was not hidden for 30 s; `connected` says whether the revision event of the open stream has arrived. A stream that streamed for 10 s and ended opens again within a second, a failure after 1 s doubling up to 60 s with jitter, a 401 or 403 (after the interceptor's one refresh) not before the session is renewed (a new sign-in or the next token refresh). A stream that streamed for 10 s and then failed starts its retries again from 1 s; each attempt of the request (`Sent`, such as the interceptor's retry after a refresh) is read from its own start. `authInterceptor` reads a problem from a text body too (`application/problem+json` as text), so the stream's 401 `auth/expired-token` refreshes like any call. Specs get `testing/live-fixtures.ts` (`FakeLiveUpdates`: `hint(feed)`, `resync()`, `connected`, `listeners()`) from `src/test-providers.ts`, so no spec opens a stream unless it asks for the real service.
- `core/updates/` and the service worker (plan v3 2.4): `angular-service-worker.ts` (`provideAppServiceWorker()`, in `app.config.ts`) registers Angular's `ngsw-worker.js` (`SERVICE_WORKER_SCRIPT`, a relative name, which the Trusted Types policy resolves against the base URL) with `registerWhenStable:30000`, off in development, and hands `SwUpdate` to the rest of the app as `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES` (`service-worker-updates.ts`, `NO_SERVICE_WORKER` without one, so specs and a browser without service workers take the same path). `app-updates.ts` (`AppUpdates.start()` from an app initializer) decides when a ready version (`VERSION_READY`) takes over: right away only within `START_UP_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) of the start, before the first `pointerdown`, `keydown`, `scroll`, `wheel` or `touchstart`, while the page is still at the address it started at (a page that changed its own address, as one that took a link code off it, counts as touched) and with no half-typed form; otherwise at the next router navigation to another path (a change of only the query or the section, `?strana=2` or `#o-nas`, stays in the app), as a full load of that address (`ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer`), and when a half-typed form is on the page the navigation runs in the app and the load follows only once the form is gone; every automatic load is one `ReloadGuard.claim('update')`. An answer naming another build than the app's (`ServerVersion.revision` against `APP_REVISION`) asks the worker to look for an update once per build. `VERSION_INSTALLATION_FAILED` reports `sw-install`/`service-worker`; `SwUpdate.unrecoverable` (a file of the tab's version gone from the server and from the cache) reports `sw-unrecoverable`/`service-worker` and reloads once (`'unrecoverable'`), or sets `mustReload`, which shows the update notice, when a form is half-typed or the guard refuses. `loadNewVersion(url)` asks the worker for the new build (at most `UPDATE_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 s, a failure ignored) and then loads `url` from the server; the update notice and `ChunkLoadRecovery` load through it. `dirty-forms.ts` (`DirtyForms.register(check)`, `any()`, a throwing check counts as dirty) and `tracked-form.ts` (`form[appTrackedForm]`, a host directive of `appFocusFirstInvalid`, so every form of the site registers `NgForm.dirty`; the administration's settings form, which has no `appFocusFirstInvalid`, carries it itself). `boot-report.ts` (`markCodeArrived(document)`, the first statement of `main.ts`, sets `data-nfnn-loading` on `<html>`, so the watchdog knows the code arrived and the start only waits; `BootReport.started()`, called by `main.ts` once the app is bootstrapped) sets `data-nfnn-started` on `<html>` and, when `public/boot-guard.js` had to step in during this tab's start (`sessionStorage` `nfnn.boot.recovered`), reports `boot`/`start-up` once. `public/boot-guard.js` (a plain script of the build, loaded by `index.html` before the app, allowed by the policy as `'self'`): when the app has not set `data-nfnn-started` 15 s after the page began, it waits in 15 s steps while `data-nfnn-loading` is set (the code arrived and the start waits on the network, such as a session refresh during an outage) up to 60 s and then shows the panel without a reload; when the code never arrived, it reloads once per 60 s (`nfnn.reload.boot`, never while `navigator.onLine` is false) and otherwise shows the full-screen panel "Web se nenačetl" (`role="alert"`, the button "Vyčistit a načíst znovu" unregisters every service worker and deletes every cache, never `localStorage`, IndexedDB or the cookies, so the sign-in and the data kept on the device stay), which goes away by itself if the app starts after all; its Czech text has no i18n ids, since it runs before Angular; `scripts/boot-guard.test.mjs` tests it in a jsdom page. The panel also offers "Stáhnout diagnostický soubor" (the help line: download it and send it to info@nfnn.cz, it holds no personal data and goes nowhere by itself): `boot-guard.js` gathers the file when the panel shows (before "Vyčistit a načíst znovu" could clear what it counts; each browser answer waits at most 2 s) and at the press shares it on an iPhone or iPad that can share files (the share sheet; a closed sheet saves nothing, a failed share saves instead) and otherwise saves it through a hidden `<a download>` on a `blob:` address revoked after the click, with a `role="status"` line naming the saved file and, when the browser refuses to make it, a `role="alert"` line that asks to write without it (the button keeps its name), built with its own ES5 copies of the closed lists, the user agent reduction and the first path segments of the routes (`core/diagnostics/` below), and keeps each start it gave up on (reloaded or shown the panel) as `nfnn.boot.last` in `sessionStorage` (`panelShown`, `codeArrived`, `waitedSeconds`, `at`), which the next file of the tab reads as `boot.previous`; the test runs every file it saves through the app's `sanitizedDiagnostics` (equal, so nothing is dropped), compares its platform with `detectPlatform` over a user agent corpus and plants canaries (`scripts/ts-import.mjs` lets Node import the schema's TypeScript). `ngsw-config.json`: the shell (`index.html`, `boot-guard.js`, the manifest, the tab icon, `icon-512.png` of the launch screen, the logos, every `main-`, `polyfills-`, `chunk-` and `styles-` file and the fonts under `/media/`) is prefetched, the workers, receipt fonts and other images are cached the first time they are asked for, every app route is a navigation served from `index.html` (never `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/.well-known/`, `/actuator/` or a file), and only public answers that are the same for everyone are kept (`public-content`, performance with `refreshAhead` 0: the home content, news and projects, served from the kept copy at once and fetched again behind it into the copy, 5 s for one never kept, 100 for 30 days; `payment-details`, freshness with 1 s, a day; `bank-logos`, performance: `/api/donations/bank-logos/images/*`, the bank logos, 60 at most for a year, listed before `bank-logo-list`, freshness with 1 s, a day, for `/api/donations/bank-logos` (the worker matches a group's pattern anywhere in the address and takes the first group that matches, so the images' group must come first); `public-photos`, performance: `/api/content/media/*`, the photos the visitor has already seen, 300 at most, Q8); `backend/.../ServiceWorkerCachedAnswersTest` proves each cached path answers alike with no token, a donor's and an administrator's and sets no cookie, and that the data group URLs find no other GET handler, and `scripts/ngsw-config.test.mjs` which built files land in which group. `service-worker-bypass.ts` (`serviceWorkerBypassInterceptor`, the innermost interceptor of `appHttpClient`) adds `ngsw-bypass: true` to every `/api` request that is not a `GET` of an anchored public answer (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`), because Angular matches a data group URL anywhere in the address; `versionInterceptor` ignores the version headers of an answer that may be a copy the worker kept (`mayComeFromServiceWorker` while `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES.isEnabled`), so a weeks-old copy never names an old generation. `core/install/app-install.ts` (`AppInstall`): `way()` is `prompt` while the browser offered `beforeinstallprompt` (kept and prevented), `ios-share` on an iPhone or iPad, `mac-dock` in Safari on a Mac, `none` once installed or when the page runs as the app (`(display-mode: standalone)` or `navigator.standalone`); `offered()` is false for 90 days after "Teď ne" (`localStorage` `nfnn.install-offer.hidden-until`). `core/auth/app-entry-guard.ts`: `/aplikace`, the manifest's `start_url` and `id`, sends a signed-in administrator to `/sprava`, a signed-in donor to `/darce/prehled` and anyone else to `/` without opening the sign-in dialog (#349: a modal at launch focused the e-mail field and raised the keyboard; the header's "Přihlásit se" offers the sign-in, and the experts of #349 kept the role-based start after the app was closed instead of reopening the last screen), after one session refresh when the `nfnn.donor-session` flag says a session may exist. The built `ngsw-worker.js` begins with the site's payment route (`frontend/service-worker/payment-route.js`, prepended by `scripts/wrap-service-worker.mjs`, the `postbuild` of `npm run build`, once, which fails when a build with `ngsw.json` keeps a worker without it; the registered address and the Trusted Types policy stay the same, and the stack probe checks that the image's worker begins with it): a `message` and a `fetch` listener registered before Angular's, which take only their own message and the same-origin path `/platba.spayd` and stop the event there, never the network and nothing under `/api/`. The message `{ type: 'nfnn-payment-file', payment }` with a SPAYD text (`SPD*1.0*` and the QR alphanumeric characters `spayd()` writes, at most 1 000 characters) reserves it in the worker's memory for 10 s under a random id (at most 16 kept, the oldest giving way), answered on the message's port (`id: null` for anything else); `GET /platba.spayd?id=<id>`, the id as its only parameter, serves that text once as `platba.spayd` (`Content-Disposition: inline`, `no-store`, `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox allow-downloads; frame-ancestors 'none'`), any other address 404 (a navigation 204, so a tab whose worker restarted between the reservation and the tap stays on the payment panel with its notice) and any other method 405, both as sandboxed plain text; so the address never carries a payment, and a link from elsewhere cannot make the site serve one. `scripts/service-worker-payment-route.test.mjs` runs the route in a VM and the wrap, and `e2e/pwa/payment-route.spec.ts` proves it behind the real nginx.
- `core/push/` (slice 2.28): push goes through Angular's own `ngsw-worker.js` and `SwPush`, never a worker of the site's own (Trusted Types admit only `/ngsw-worker.js`). `service-worker-push.ts` holds the token `SERVICE_WORKER_PUSH` (`NO_PUSH` by default, so development, specs and a browser without a worker take one path) with `isEnabled`, `controllerChanges`, `messages`, `notificationClicks` (`{action, data}`), `requestPermission()` (asks `Notification.requestPermission()` at once, so it runs inside the click's gesture, and answers `granted`, `denied` or `default`), `requestSubscription(publicKey)` → `{endpoint, p256dh, auth}` (`devicePushSubscriptionOf`, from `PushSubscription.toJSON().keys`, refused without them; a subscription the browser still holds under another `applicationServerKey`, as after the server replaced its push key, is ended first, because the browser refuses to subscribe again over it), `browserSubscription()` (`subscribed`, `not-subscribed` or `unknown`) and `unsubscribe()` (a device that was not subscribed counts as done). The last two and the check before subscribing read `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` → `pushManager.getSubscription()` themselves instead of `SwPush`, whose calls wait for a worker that controls the page and so never settle after a hard reload or with the server's switch off; each gives up after `PUSH_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s), so a button that waits on one always settles; `core/updates/angular-service-worker.ts`, the only file that imports `@angular/service-worker`, provides it from `SwPush`, enabled only while a worker controls the page and the browser has `PushManager` and `Notification`. ngsw itself shows the notification of a message (`notification` of the payload) and on a click focuses the open app or opens the payload's `onActionClick` address. `alert-routes.ts` is the app's own closed table of the kinds (`test` → `/sprava/upozorneni`, `bank-import-*` → `/sprava`, `backups-*` → `/sprava/zalohy`, `mail-*` → `/sprava/nastaveni`, anything else → `/sprava/upozorneni`) and reads a message's `count` (a whole number 0–999, `MAX_OPEN_ALERT_COUNT`, the backend's `PushMessage.MAX_COUNT`, else nothing); its kinds are the shared cases `shared-cases/push/notification.json` (kind → tag and route), which `alert-routes.spec.ts` runs and `e2e/push-device.ts` reads its messages from. `alert-notification-clicks.ts` (`AlertNotificationClicks.start()`) routes a click the open app hears with `Router.navigateByUrl` by the kind alone, never to an address taken from the message, so the unsaved-changes guard still asks, and only in the tab that is visible and has the focus (ngsw focuses one tab and then tells every tab, so the others stay where they are); `app-badge.ts` (`AppBadge.start()`, `show(count)`) sets `navigator.setAppBadge(count)` or `clearAppBadge()` for each message with a count (the test alert carries the open count too) and when the alerts page loads, feature-detected and never throwing, and clears the badge when the signed-in administrator signs out or loses the role (`AuthStore.isAdmin` turning false). ngsw never sets a badge, so a closed app's badge shows what it knew when it was last open: the administration shell refreshes it (`features/admin/shell/alert-badge-refresh.ts`, `AlertBadgeRefresh.watch`) with one `GET /api/admin/operations/push` when the shell starts and again when the tab becomes visible, at most once a minute (`ALERT_BADGE_REFRESH_GAP_MS`), a failure ignored. Both start from an app initializer in `app.config.ts`. `push-availability.ts` tells the alerts page why a device cannot subscribe.
- `core/errors/`: `error-vocabulary.ts` holds the closed lists a browser error report may carry (`ERROR_KINDS` `unexpected`, `chunk-load`, `pdf-render`, `pdf-load`, `qr-encode`, `verify-parse`, `verify-signature`, `boot`, `sw-install`, `sw-unrecoverable`; `ERROR_PLACES` `global`, `navigation`, `start-up`, `service-worker`; `ERROR_NAMES`, the standard error and `DOMException` names plus `HttpErrorResponse` and `Other`; `BROWSERS`, `OPERATING_SYSTEMS`, `DISPLAY_MODES`), and `npm run errors:vocabulary` (`scripts/export-error-vocabulary.mjs`) writes them to `error-vocabulary.json`, which the spec keeps equal to the TypeScript and the backend's `ClientErrorVocabularyTest` to its enums. `client-platform.ts` (`detectPlatform`) turns the user agent into a browser family, its major version (1–999, else none) and an OS family, nothing more; `app-revision.ts` reads `NFNN_APP_REVISION`, which the image's build defines (`--define`), and keeps it only when it is what the server accepts (`revisionOf`: 7–40 lowercase hex digits or `dev`), `dev` otherwise, so a build with an odd revision never makes every report a 400. `error-reporter.ts` (`ErrorReporter.report(kind, place, error?)`) never throws: it keeps the error's `name` only when it is in `ERROR_NAMES` (`Other` otherwise) and never reads its message or stack, sends one report per kind, place and name every 10 minutes and counts the repeats it held back into the next one's `count` (at most 100), sends at most 10 per page load, keeps up to 20 while `Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()` says offline and sends them on the window's `online` event, and posts with a raw `fetch` (`keepalive`, `credentials: 'omit'`, no interceptor, a failure ignored) to `/api/operations/client-errors`. `reporting-error-handler.ts`: `ReportingErrorHandler` extends Angular's `ErrorHandler`, keeps its console output and reports `unexpected`/`global`, except a failed chunk (`isChunkLoadError`), which `ChunkLoadRecovery` reports itself, so a lost chunk is one report per page load; `app.config.ts` provides it as `appErrorHandler` (`app.config.spec.ts` checks `appConfig.providers` holds it). `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` reports `chunk-load`/`navigation` for a failed chunk. The receipt PDF (`ReceiptPdf`: `pdf-load`, `pdf-render`) and the verification page (`ReceiptVerifier`: `verify-signature`; `QrReader`: `qr-encode`) report their failures with place `global` (below); their specs assert the exact closed body through `core/errors/testing/client-error-reports.ts`. Specs: `error-reporter.spec.ts` (the canary `Error('Zxqv Kanárek 7781,77 Kč 2099-778177')` arrives as closed values only, dedupe, the per-load limit, the offline queue, the allowlist), `client-platform.spec.ts`, `error-vocabulary.spec.ts`, `reporting-error-handler.spec.ts`; `app-revision.spec.ts`; Playwright `e2e/client-errors.spec.ts` (an error thrown on the privacy page posts one report of closed values; a lost chunk posts exactly one `chunk-load` report per page load and no `unexpected` one; a receipt font gone after a deploy posts one `pdf-load` report named `NotFoundError`, and the page loads once more and finishes the download).
- `core/files/file-download.ts` `saveBlob(file)` (a `SavedFile`, `{ blob, fileName }`; the receipt PDF, the payment file, backups, the backup key and the diagnostic file save through it): object URL on a hidden `<a download>` clicked inside the page, revoked in `setTimeout(…, 0)` (Safari and Firefox cancel a synchronously revoked download).
- `core/diagnostics/` (the diagnostic file, Jakub 2026-11-06; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Diagnostic file (in the browser)"): `diagnostics-schema.ts` (no Angular, so `scripts/read-diagnostics.mjs` and the boot watchdog's test import it too) holds the file's schema, `DiagnosticsFile`, and `sanitizedDiagnostics(raw)`, which builds the file from its fixed paths only, never copying or spreading its input, and turns every leaf outside its closed list, pattern or range into `null` (an event outside the lists is dropped, a `detail` is kept only for its own kind, at most the 50 latest events), with `pageOf(path)` (the route pattern of `DIAGNOSTIC_PAGES`, kept equal to the nginx routes by its spec, else `other`), `ageBucket`, `createdAtOf` and `diagnosticsFileName` (`nfnn-diagnostika-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<HHMM>.json`, UTC). `diagnostic-events.ts` (`DiagnosticEvents`, root) keeps the events of this page in memory only, at most 50 of the last 24 h: `ErrorReporter.report(kind, place, error?, detail?)` records every report, also those its repeat window or per-load cap holds back, with its closed `detail` (the receipt PDF's failure reason), which is never sent, and `DonorCopySync` records each sync's outcome as `copy-sync`; `AuthStore` empties it when a signed-in donor's session ends and when another account signs in. `device-copy-diagnostics.ts`: the token `DEVICE_COPY_DIAGNOSTICS` (`null` by default; `app.config.ts` asks `DeviceCopies.diagnostics()`, which answers `null` until a session loaded the vault and then `DeviceVault.diagnostics()`: mode, donor copy `none`/`open`/`locked`, age bucket of the stored copy, administration copy `none`/`locked`/`open`). `diagnostics.ts` (`Diagnostics.file(source)` and `download(source)`) gathers the closed state (the revision, the start attributes, `platformOf` of `client-platform.ts`, the page, `Connectivity`, feature support, `persisted` and `nearlyFull` from the storage estimate, service worker and cache counts, the ages of `nfnn.reload.*` and `nfnn.boot.last`, the session's kind and account `none`/`donor`/`administrator`, only `signed-in` and no administration state in a `PUBLIC` session, the device copy and the events), each browser answer waiting at most 2 s, and `prepare` returns the JSON as a blob with its name, `save` saves it through `saveBlob`, and `needsSecondTap` and `share` hand it to the share sheet on an iPhone or iPad (`DIAGNOSTICS_NAVIGATOR`). ESLint refuses in this folder reading a donor, a token, an e-mail, a name, an open copy or its contents, the address beyond its path, the title, a cookie, the referrer, the client hints, `localStorage`, an error's message or stack (as a member, by brackets or by destructuring), any `setItem`, `removeItem`, `clear()` or assignment on `sessionStorage` and spreading an object (`scripts/diagnostics-confinement.test.mjs`). Specs: `diagnostics-schema.spec.ts` (exact keys, forged values, `__proto__`, the routes), `diagnostic-events.spec.ts`, `diagnostics.spec.ts` (canaries in the path, query and fragment, title, cookie, both storages, an error and an HTTP error, the donor's name and e-mail, the user agent, a cache name, a service worker scope and script, the referrer, `/overit#…` and `/pripojeni?…`, found by `raw-scan.ts`'s `textDump` and `leaks` in plain, URL-encoded, base64 and hex form; the client hints never read; nothing written in a `PUBLIC` session).
- `app.routes.ts`: `''` → `layout/public-shell` (header, footer) with children `'obnova-hesla'` → `features/donors/password-reset/` (title "Nové heslo | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'potvrzeni-emailu'` → `features/donors/email-verification/` (title "Potvrzení e-mailu | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'pripojeni'` → `features/donors/connect-app/` (title "Připojení aplikace | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'prvni-nastaveni'` → `features/operations/setup/first-setup` (title "První nastavení | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'overit'` → `features/donations/verification/verification-page` (title "Ověření potvrzení o daru | Nová naděje – nadační fond") and `''` → `features/content/content.routes.ts` (`''` home, `'zasady-ochrany-osobnich-udaju'` privacy policy, `'aktuality'` and `'projekty'` → `features/content/list/content-list` with route data `kind` (titles "Aktuality | Nová naděje – nadační fond", "Kde pomáháme | …"), `'aktuality/:slug'` and `'projekty/:slug'` → `features/content/detail/content-detail` with route data `kind` `news` / `project` (the tab title becomes "<title> | Nová naděje – nadační fond" once loaded), `'**'` not-found; a route cannot have both `children` and `loadChildren`, so the content routes hang under a child of their own); `'darce'` → `layout/donor-shell` with `features/donors/donors.routes.ts` behind `core/auth/donor-guard.ts`: one parent `''` → `features/donors/dashboard/donor-area` (the donor area) with the sections `'prehled'` (title "Můj přehled | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'dary'` ("Moje dary | …"), `'potvrzeni'` ("Potvrzení o daru | …"), `'ucty'` ("Bankovní účty | …"), `'udaje'` ("Údaje a zabezpečení | …") and `''` redirecting to `'prehled'`; `'sprava'` → the same `donor-shell` with `features/admin/admin.routes.ts` (`''` → `features/admin/home/`, title "Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'platby'` → `features/admin/payments/`, title "Platby z banky | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'spravci'` → `features/admin/administrators/`, title "Správci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'starsi-symboly'` → `features/admin/symbol-claims/`, title "Odpovědi o starších symbolech | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'loga-bank'` → `features/admin/bank-logos/`, title "Loga bank | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'historie'` → `features/admin/actions/`, title "Historie změn | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'prehledy'` → `features/admin/report/`, title "Měsíční e-mail | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'zalohy'` → `features/admin/backups/`, title "Zálohy | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'upozorneni'` → `features/admin/alerts/`, title "Upozornění | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'logy'` → `features/admin/logs/`, title "Logy a diagnostika | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'nastaveni'` → `features/admin/settings/`, title "Nastavení | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news`, title "Aktuality | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality/nova'` and `'aktuality/:id'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news-editor`, titles "Nová aktualita | Správa | …" and "Úprava aktuality | Správa | …"; `'projekty'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-projects`, title "Pomáháme | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'projekty/nova'` and `'projekty/:id'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-project-editor`, titles "Nový příběh | Správa | …" and "Úprava příběhu | Správa | …"; `'darci'` → `features/admin/donors/`, title "Dárci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'darci/:id'` → `features/admin/donor-detail/`, title "Dárce | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond") behind `core/auth/admin-guard.ts`. `'donor'` redirects to `'darce'` with the rest of the path (the earlier English address). Every address a visitor sees is Czech without diacritics; only `/api/...` stays English. Unknown addresses keep their URL and show `not-found`. nginx answers exactly these addresses with the app and status 200 (the map `$app_page` of `frontend/nginx.conf`, one line per route, a parameter as `[^/]+`, no trailing slash, because the router shows `not-found` for `/o-nas/` too) and every other path that is no file with the same `index.html` and status 404 (issue #350, Jakub 2026-11-06: nothing changes for the visitor, only the status is right); `app.routes.spec.ts` walks these routes (lazy children loaded, a prefix redirect such as `'donor'` taking the target's addresses) and fails unless the list equals the map, read by `scripts/app-routes.mjs` through the global setup `src/testing/server-routes-setup.ts`, so a new route adds its line there in the same change.
- `core/device/store/` (plan v3, 2.14; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies"): `device-storage.ts` (the `DeviceStorage` port: device salt, registry entries, session keys and sealed records, every `write(change)` atomic; the database `nfnn.device`), `idb-device-storage.ts` (the only importer of idb, `eslint-confinement.js`; four object stores, one `readwrite` transaction per change, opening given 5 s, `blocking` closes the connection so another tab can delete the database, `destroy()` deletes it; `idbDeviceStoragePresent()` asks `indexedDB.databases()` so a sign-out never makes an empty database), `memory-device-storage.ts` (the same in memory, for memory-only mode), `account-store.ts` (`AccountStore` of one open copy and feed: `load()` → `empty`, `loaded` or `damaged`, `replace(contents)` seals one record per chunk and one for the summary under random record ids, the signal `contents`), `device-vault.ts` (`DeviceVault`, root; from 2.19 also the administration's copy: `administration` (the open copy, `reason` `sign-in`, `empty` or `unlocked`), `stores`, `administrationPasskeys`, `unlockAdministrationWithPasskey` (the PRF ceremony through `PASSKEY_READER`, outside the vault lock, and nothing opens when a sign-in or sign-out came in between), `unlockAdministrationWithPassword`, `lockAdministration`, `administrationRevoked` (deletes the administration copy and keeps the donor's), closed whenever `AdministrationAccess` locks; `signedIn`, `restored`, `signedOut`, `signedOutElsewhere`, `sessionLost`, `forget`, `passwordChanged`, `passwordRemoved`, `passkeyAdded`, `prfInput`, `replaceContents`, `withCopyLock`; `signedIn` and `restored` take `storeNothing`, which `AuthStore` sets for a `PUBLIC` session (2.10), so a "Cizí počítač" copy lives only in memory, also after a reload; signals `opened` (`{ store, reason: 'sign-in' | 'restored' | 'empty', account }`), `mode` (`stored` | `memory`) and `locked` (a copy of the account is on the device but this session cannot open it); tokens `DEVICE_STORAGE_OPENER`, `DEVICE_STORAGE_PRESENT`, `PASSWORD_KEY_DERIVATION`, `COPY_CHANNELS`, `STORAGE_PERSISTENCE`; Web Locks `nfnn.device-vault` and `nfnn.device-copy-write.<id>`, `BroadcastChannel` `nfnn.device-copy.<id>`, `localStorage` flag `nfnn.device-copy`), `testing/raw-scan.ts` (every stored value as text, its bytes and its base64, hex and URL decodings, for the canaries) and `testing/fake-device.ts` (named Web Locks and a channel bus for two tabs in one spec). `core/auth/session-device.ts` is what `AuthStore` tells the device (`SESSION_DEVICE`, a no-op by default, so specs and pages without the vault change nothing; `deviceAccountOf(donor)` with the passkey user handle of the donor's id and `administrator` for the `ADMIN` role); `core/auth/administration-access.ts` (`AdministrationAccess`, root, 2.19: locked at every start, unlocked by a sign-in in this tab and by the lock panel, locked again by sign-out and after `ADMINISTRATION_LOCK_AFTER_MS`, five minutes, with the page hidden (`ACCESS_CLOCK` reads both `performance.now()` and `Date.now()`, and either clock having moved five minutes, or the wall clock having gone back, locks it, because the monotonic clock stops while Apple devices, Android and Linux sleep; checked again on `visibilitychange`, `resume` and `pageshow`), `keepsPage` set when a form is dirty as it locks); the app provides `device-copies.ts` (`DeviceCopies`), which loads the vault and `features/donations/device-copy/` with `import()` at the first session event, so neither is in the bundle that loads first, or already when the sign-in dialog opens (`prepare()`, 2.19), because a passkey sign-in waits only 1.5 s for the device's PRF input and a copy signed in without it gets no passkey wrap. `features/donations/device-copy/`: `donor-sync-api.ts` (`/api/donations/me/sync` and `/sync/chunks?ids=`, at most 64 ids), `donor-copy-sync.ts` (`DonorCopySync`, 2.15: syncs a copy whenever one opens, at sign-in, restored or empty, and on `sync()`, `reload()` (every chunk again) and `catchUp()` (nothing within 30 s of the last sync, and it joins a sync that runs); one sync at a time and at most one queued after it, which a `reload()` turns into a full one; under the copy lock it reads the stored copy, asks for the manifest and only the chunks whose digest changed, checks every digest with `canonical-rows.ts`, tries twice, gives each request 30 s (`SYNC_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, so the copy lock is never held for ever), refuses an answer whose `subject` is not the open copy's donor (unreported: the session changed, not the data), keeps the signature and portrait of every active receipt (fetched through `ReceiptsApi.image` and kept only when they give their digest) and stores the whole copy with `DeviceVault.replaceContents`; then compares the copy's own figures with its summary, downloads it whole again at most once an hour when they differ and reports `device-copy-mismatch` at most once an hour (both pauses kept in memory, so per open tab); signals `syncing`, `open`, `view`, `firstSync` and `keepsOnDevice`), `donor-copy.ts` (`DonorCopyLink`, root: the `DonorCopy` the pages read, set by `DonorCopySync` when `device-copies.ts` loads it, so the donor pages reach the copy without importing the vault), `donor-copy-view.ts` (pure: `donorCopyView(contents)` turns the stored chunks into the summary, the gifts as `DonationItem`s in the order the API lists them (purpose text, sender account by `bankAccountId`, confirmed symbol by `symbolClaimId`, receipt number of the active single receipt, `receiptAvailable`), the issued receipts, the kept images and `agrees`; `summarizeDonations` adds up gifts as the server's summary does; `activeReceiptOf`, `imageDigestsOf`) and `copy-receipt-source.ts` (`copyBackedReceiptSource`: a receipt is issued online as before, and only when the server cannot be reached (status 0, or a gateway status without a problem body) the copy's active receipt of that year or gift is drawn; an image the copy keeps is read from it, any other from the server; the PDF is drawn again each time and never stored), `donor-donations-repository.ts` (`DonorDonationsRepository`, the one read of the donor's gifts for the pages, Donor pages below) and `testing/fake-donor-copy.ts` (`FakeDonorCopy` and `copyViewOf` for the page specs).
- `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (above) and `core/auth/`: `donor.ts` (DTOs `Donor`, `RegisterDonorRequest`, `AuthResponse`, `PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH` 12 shared by registration and reset), `auth-api.ts` (`/api/donors/auth/{register,login,login/second-factor,login/second-factor/recovery,refresh,logout}`, `email-verification/{request,confirm}`, `password-reset/{request,confirm}`, `/api/donors/me`, `/api/donors/me/password`, `/api/donors/me/email-change`, `/api/donors/me/second-factor/{setup,confirm,disable}`, `describeClient` → `/api/donors/oauth/client`, `authorize` → `/api/donors/oauth/authorize`, `connections` and `disconnect` → `/api/donors/me/connections[/{id}]`; `passkeySignInOptions` and `signInWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/passkey[/options]`, `passkeys`, `passkeyCreationOptions(confirmation)`, `addPasskey` and `removePasskey` → `/api/donors/me/passkeys[/options|/{id}]`, `removePassword` → `/api/donors/me/password/remove`, `passkeyRecoveryOptions` and `recoverWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/password-reset/passkey[/options]` (DTOs `PasskeyCreationOptions`, `PasskeySignInOptions`, `PasskeyRegistrationCredential`, `PasskeyAssertionCredential`, `Passkey`); the DTOs `ClientDescription`, `AuthorizationRequest`, `RedirectResponse`, `ConnectedApp`, `OAuthScope` in `donor.ts`), `auth-store.ts` (signals `donor`, `status`, `signedIn`, `isAdmin` (the session's roles contain `ADMIN`, which the backend grants only after the second factor); `register` opens no session, `confirmEmail(token)`, `completeSecondFactor(challenge, code)`, `changePassword`, `confirmSecondFactor(code)`, `disableSecondFactor` and `signInWithPasskey()` (options → the device → the answer) do, `setupSecondFactor(confirmation)` only returns the enrolment, `removePassword(confirmation)` adopts the new session, `confirmWith(password | null)` turns the password, or for an account without one the device's sign-in prompt over `/api/donors/auth/passkey/options`, into the `Confirmation` the credential changes send, `requestEmailChange` changes nothing until the link is opened; access token in memory; one refresh shared between callers, and between the tabs of the browser (plan v3, 2.8): the refresh runs under the Web Lock `nfnn-refresh` (`session-tabs.ts`: `TAB_LOCKS` is `navigator.locks`, null where the browser has none, which then refreshes without it), a restore gives up at once when `Connectivity` says the app is offline, before it asks, or when it goes offline while the restore waits (`wentOffline`); a restore whose refresh has no answer after 1 s (`CONNECTION_CHECK_AFTER_MS`) also asks `Connectivity.tryAgain()`, so a network that hangs ends it within about 6 s and the visitor waits signed out with the hint kept, and the next restore asks again; its request is cut off after 15 s (`REFRESH_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, the `XMLHttpRequest` aborted before the lock is released, so no other tab refreshes with the cookie a late answer would still replace) and a tab gives up waiting for the lock after 20 s (`REFRESH_LOCK_WAIT_MS`, longer than the request); the tab that refreshed announces the new session on the `BroadcastChannel` `nfnn-session` (`SESSION_CHANNEL`, a `session-refreshed` message of `version` 1 with the `AuthResponse`, checked by `isSessionRefreshed`, which also asks for the user's id and roles and the expiry, so a tab of another build after a deploy changes nothing with a message it would misread; posting never fails the refresh; the channel closed with the store), every other tab adopts it unless it holds the session of another donor; a refresh, once started, runs to its answer or its timeout even when its caller is gone (`share({ resetOnRefCountZero: false })`), because an aborted answer would be a lost one; `logout()` runs under the same lock (or, after 20 s of waiting, without it), its request cut off after 15 s like a refresh's so a stalled sign-out never holds the other tabs' refresh, then forgets the session and posts `signed-out`, on which every other tab forgets its own, and a tab that waited for the lock and meanwhile got one sends no refresh of its own (one that did not still presents the cookie the first tab's answer set, so the server sees a plain rotation); only a refusal (a 4xx other than 408 and 429) ends the session in the tab and drops the flag, and nothing else the browser keeps; a refresh without an answer (no connection, the timeout, the lock wait) keeps the session and the flag, and a `restore()` that got none leaves the visitor anonymous until the next `restore()`, which tries again at once after a quick failure and only after 10 s (`SLOW_RESTORE_PAUSE_MS`) after a timeout or a lock wait that gave up, so the guard of the first page does not keep a blank page waiting a second time; `restore()` on start calls `refresh` only when the `localStorage` flag `nfnn.donor-session` says a session may exist; session kinds (plan v3 2.10): `session-jar.ts` gives `SESSION_JAR`, the browser's jar (`nfnn-refresh`, `nfnn-session`, `nfnn.donor-session`) or, when the app runs installed (`runsInstalled`: `display-mode: standalone` or `navigator.standalone`), the app's (`nfnn-refresh-app`, `nfnn-session-app`, `nfnn.donor-session-app`), so the installed app and a tab of the same browser keep their own lock, channel and flag, and `auth-api.ts` sends `Nfnn-Session: APP` on the app's refresh and sign-out, so the backend reads and clears the app's own cookie; `login`, `completeSecondFactor` and `signInWithPasskey` take the kind the dialog chose (`BROWSER`, `REMEMBERED` or `PUBLIC`; the app always sends `APP`, an e-mail confirmation `BROWSER`), and the store keeps the answer's `session()`; a `PUBLIC` session keeps its flag in `sessionStorage` instead, so nothing of it outlives the browser, and `storesNothing()` tells the device copy (2.14) to keep nothing on the device; because `sessionStorage` belongs to one tab, a tab opened from a public-computer session starts signed out and offers the sign-in again, which is accepted for a borrowed computer), `auth-interceptor.ts` (Bearer on `/api/**` except the auth endpoints and a request whose `HttpContext` sets `WITHOUT_ACCESS_TOKEN` (the public receipt verification, the payment details and the setup status), which goes out without the header and is never refreshed; parses an `application/problem+json` Blob body into the `HttpErrorResponse` first; on 401 `auth/expired-token` (and, since 2.9, `auth/session-ended`, a token of a device signed out elsewhere, whose refresh then fails and forgets the session, a sign-out for the device copy, 2.14) refreshes once and retries; a request whose `HttpContext` sets `WITHOUT_ACCESS_TOKEN` goes without the Bearer header and so never refreshes, which `payment-details-api.ts` uses because that public answer is the same for everyone and the backend answers an expired token with 401 even there), `donor-guard.ts` (waits for the restore, else goes home and opens the sign-in dialog with `returnTo` set to the asked address), `login-dialog.service.ts` (`open(mode = 'login', { returnTo, afterSignIn, email })`, `LoginDialogMode` `login` | `register` | `reset`, read once by the dialog as its initial tab, `email` prefilling that tab's address (the first setup passes the administrator's); `keepRegistrationDraft`/`registrationDraft`/`dropRegistrationDraft` hold the registration fields (never the password) of a dialog closed half way; the dialog calls `signedIn()` after a sign-in (with or without the second factor), which closes it and then opens `returnTo` when given, else stays on the page and calls `afterSignIn` (Jakub 2026-10-23: a sign-in from the home page stays there, the header then offers "Můj přehled"; only a sign-in asked for by a protected page returns to it). `returnTo` comes from the guards, from the session-loss handlers of the overview and of every administration page (`router.url` before going home) and from the password-reset page (`/darce/prehled`)), `passkey-authenticator.ts` (`PasskeyAuthenticator.supported()`, `create(options)` and `get(options)` over `navigator.credentials` with base64url both ways; a refusal becomes `PasskeyCeremonyError` with `cancelled` (`NotAllowedError`, `AbortError`), `already-registered` (`InvalidStateError`), `unsupported` or `failed`; specs replace it with `testing/FakePasskeyAuthenticator`), `device-name.ts` (`deviceName(document)`: iPhone, iPad, Android, Mac, Windows, Chromebook or Linux from the user agent, the default name of a new key), `core/navigation/external-navigation.ts` (`ExternalNavigation.assign(url)` leaves the app for another site; specs replace it), `api-problem.ts` (`describeProblem(failure, generic)`: title, detail and field messages in one line, or `generic`; `problemOf` also reads the second-factor `challenge`), `testing/` fixtures (`sampleDonor`, `secondFactorRequired`, `secondFactorEnrolment`).
- Bounds on client work (`.claude/rules/reliability.md`): the bank check polls for at most 90 s, read again on the live `bank-import` hint, else every 2 s without a stream and 15 s with one; the payment panel's watch for an arriving gift (`gift-arrival/`) asks the bank only after an intent to pay, 30, 60, 120, 240 and 480 s after it and at once on a return to the tab, at least 30 s apart, at most 12 times and for 10 minutes after the last intent or return, never in a hidden tab or offline; the add-account watch makes at most 20 checks 30 s apart, paused while the tab is hidden; the backup job is polled 20 × 3 s; the restart wait 100 × 3 s and the restore wait 300 × 3 s; the key card's wait for a new signing key after a withdrawal 100 × 3 s. The live stream (`core/live/`, 2.16) runs only in a visible tab with a listening page and is reopened after 1 s, doubling to 60 s with jitter, never after a 401 or 403 until the session is renewed (a new sign-in or the next token refresh). The boot watchdog (slice 2.4) waits 15 s, re-arms in 15 s steps up to 60 s while the app's code has arrived, then shows its panel; the app reloads by itself at most once per 60 s per reason (`ReloadGuard` in `sessionStorage`: `boot`, `update`, `unrecoverable`, `chunk-load`), never without the storage; a new version reloads by itself only within `START_UP_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) of the start, and `loadNewVersion` waits at most `UPDATE_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) for the worker's check. The service worker serves a kept public content answer at once and fetches it again behind it (5 s for one it never kept), gives the network 1 s before it serves the kept payment details (only on a network failure or that timeout, never on a 5xx) and keeps at most 100 public answers for 30 days, the payment details for a day and 300 photos for 365 days. A page transition covers the page for at most 5 s while the next page loads (`PAGE_COVER_LONGEST_MS`) and moves for at most 2 s (`PAGE_TRANSITION_LONGEST_MS`), then the live page shows whatever its copies still do. The connectivity probe gives up after 5 s, and a session restore waits for no network the app knows is gone. A session gets one silent refresh, then a clean sign-in. A receipt drawing that fails is reported to `/api/operations/client-errors` (1.20); the error reports also name a start the watchdog had to rescue (`boot` / `start-up`), a new version that failed to install (`sw-install`) and a running version the server no longer has (`sw-unrecoverable`, both `service-worker`).

## Shared UI (`shared/ui/`)

- `form-field/`: `<app-form-field label [hint] [messages]>` around one control marked `appFormControl` (needs `id` and `ngModel`); renders `<label for>`, the hint and the message for the first error key (or the error's own text when `messages` has no entry for it, as the server's `{ server: message }`), sets `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` once touched or submitted (touched during a pointer press only from `pointerup` or `pointercancel` on, so a message that appears on the blur cannot push the submit button away before the click; a message already shown stays during a press; a required field the visitor only passed through without typing says nothing until the form is sent: its own blur marks it untouched again, so the message cannot move the form while the next field is tapped (on iOS the press ends before the focus moves, so the press guard cannot help there), while a field the app marks touched itself, as `placeServerErrors` or a refused publish date do, shows it; with the on-screen keyboard open (`SoftKeyboard.isOpen()`), Enter in a field with `enterkeyhint="next"` moves the focus to the next text field of the form instead of sending it, unless the whole form is already valid, as after the password manager filled it in; with a hardware keyboard Enter sends the form as on any web page (#359, #384)); `form[appFocusFirstInvalid]` focuses the first invalid control on submit; `server-errors.ts` `placeServerErrors(form, errors, controlNames)` puts the field errors of a 400 problem on their controls (touched, cleared by the next edit) and hands back those without a field. Control look is the global `.form-control` in `src/styles/_form-controls.scss`; components only space fields.
- `password-reveal/`: eye button "Zobrazit heslo" (`aria-pressed`, `aria-controls`) switching the input between `password` and `text`; every password field uses it.
- `account-username/`: `<app-account-username [email]>` is a hidden, read-only `input type="email" autocomplete="username"` (`tabindex="-1"`, `aria-hidden`) holding the account's e-mail. Every form that asks for the current password without an e-mail field (the account card's e-mail change, password change, password removal and second factor on and off, the passkeys panel's new key) puts it before its first field, as Chrome and Safari ask of such forms: a password manager then knows the account the password belongs to and fills no other field (Safari ignores `autocomplete="off"` and took "Název klíče" for the user name, Jakub 2026-10-31).
- `live-notice/`: `<app-live-notice [notice]>` takes a `Notice` (`{ kind: 'status' | 'alert', text }` or `null`) and keeps a `role="status"` and a `role="alert"` region rendered, showing the text as `.ui-notice--status` or `--alert` in the matching one, so a screen reader announces it; every admin page's action line uses it. `look="note"` shows the same text as a `.ui-group-note--status` or `--alert` line under a group of rows instead of a block (the alerts page).
- `copy-button/`: `<app-copy-button [value] what="číslo účtu">`: "Kopírovat" plus the hidden `what` (an optional `label` replaces both with a visible label of its own, as "Zkopírovat zprávu pro technika"); says "Zkopírováno" for 2 s and in a `role="status"` line (an optional `copiedStatus` gives that line a sentence of its own); a visible sentence when the browser refuses (`failedStatus` for one of its own), under the button aligned to the right (`align="start"`: to the left). Its look is the global `.ui-pill-button` (`--done` while it says "Zkopírováno"), which `share-button` shares.
- `share-button/`: `<app-share-button [pageTitle] [url]>` (v3 2.26a): where the browser has the Web Share API and `navigator.canShare({ title, url })` does not refuse (or is missing), one "Sdílet" pill with a share icon that calls `navigator.share({ title, url })`, ignoring presses while the share sheet is open (`aria-disabled`); the visitor closing the sheet (`AbortError`) says nothing, any other refusal shows the `role="alert"` line "Sdílení se nepodařilo. Odkaz na stránku si můžete zkopírovat." with the copy button beside it, both kept until a share succeeds (so trying again and closing the sheet keeps the offer); each refusal renders the alert as a new element (an `@for` keyed by the count of refusals), so a screen reader hears a second refusal too, even one the browser answers at once. One `<app-copy-button>` serves both the fallback and the offer after a refusal. Without the API, or when `canShare` refuses, only `<app-copy-button>` "Kopírovat odkaz", whose status line says "Odkaz je zkopírovaný, můžete ho vložit do zprávy." and, when the browser refuses to copy, "Zkopírovat se nepodařilo. Odkaz najdete v adresním řádku prohlížeče." (the link is not written on the page), aligned to the left. Nothing loads from elsewhere and the backend is not asked. In a list of articles (v3 2.26) `[subject]` names the article: the Sdílet button and the copy button carry it as visually hidden text after their label ("Sdílet Zahrada pro děti"; the copy button's `labelDetail`), so every button of a feed has a name of its own, and `tone="dark"` (host class `is-on-dark`, also on `copy-button` as `tone`) puts its status and refusal lines in the on-image colour over a photo. Texts under `ui.share.*`; spec `share-button.spec.ts`.
- `qr-code/`: `<app-qr-code [matrix] [variant] label>` draws a QR code from a module matrix (`qr-matrix.ts`: `QrMatrix`, `isDark`, `alignmentCentres`), the browser's own from `qr-encoder.ts` for every QR code (since 1.4 the backend sends none), as inline SVG with a 4-module quiet zone, `role="img"` named by `label`. `branded` (default, the payment look chosen by Jakub on 2026-10-23): round dots in `--color-qr-module`, rounded finder squares and alignment marks in `--color-qr-mark`, and over the centre a blue disc with the logo's star (`--color-logo`, `--color-logo-fill`; the matrix must be level H); when the matrix changes it morphs in a wave from the star outwards (kept dots pulse, gone ones shrink, new ones grow in the mark colour and settle to the module colour, ~2.6 s, Web Animations API, nothing under `prefers-reduced-motion`). `plain`: black squares, used for the authenticator link. `pngDataUrl(sidePx)` paints a copy with the token colours and returns a PNG made on a canvas. `qr-encoder.ts` is the browser's own QR encoder (no library), bit for bit the symbol ZXing 3.5.4 drew on the backend with the ISO-8859-1 hint (the backend checked it until 1.10 retired ZXing; the shared cases keep what it drew): `encodeText(text, level)` picks the mode as ZXing does (only digits numeric, the 45 alphanumeric characters alphanumeric, anything else ISO-8859-1 bytes behind the ECI 1 header; a character outside ISO-8859-1 is refused instead of written as `?`), takes the smallest version that holds the text at the level asked for (none above 40: refused), and keeps the first of the eight masks with the lowest ZXing penalty (rule 3 counts a finder-like run only when four light modules inside the symbol lie on one side; the edge is not light); `encodeSegments(segments, level)` takes segments built by hand (`byteSegment`, `numericSegment`, `alphanumericSegment`, `eciSegment`), for the verification link of a receipt (its prefix in bytes, its payload in digits, the level chosen by the receipt). It returns the matrix with its version, level and mask; about 3 ms for an authenticator link (version 9), about 40 ms at version 40. A text over 7089 characters, more than any QR code holds, is refused before any work. Besides the payment and authenticator cases it runs `shared-cases/qr/encoder.json`, 112 symbols ZXing encoded at every level, at versions 1 to 40 and in every mode, which the backend runs against ZXing too.
- `side-nav/`: `<app-side-nav [groups] label [navId] [panel]>` renders an area's two-column layout: a sticky panel left (`--side-nav-width` 240 px, below the top safe area, the whole layout at most `--side-nav-layout-max` 1320 px) with a `nav` (`aria-label` = `label`) of `SideNavGroup`s (`{ id, label | null, items: SideNavItem[] }`; a titled group's `ul` is `aria-labelledby` its title, an untitled one has no label) of `SideNavItem` links (`{ path, label, exact?, badge? }`, `routerLinkActive` with `aria-current="page"`, prefix match unless `exact`; a `badge` `{ count, description }` with a count above 0 adds an accent pill with the count (`aria-hidden`) and the `description` as visually hidden text, so the area passes `groups` as a signal-computed value when the count changes), and the projected content (`<ng-content />`, a `router-outlet`) right. With `[panel]="false"` (an area shell passes `!CompactLayout.isCompact()`) only the projected content stays, in the same `main#obsah`, so a page is not made again when the width crosses `nav`; the panel holds only links and no menu button (since 2.24 phones reach the sections from the account sheet in the donor area and from the overview's rows in the administration).
- `radio-group/`: `[appRadioGroup]` on a group of `role="radio"` buttons makes it a `radiogroup` that answers the arrow keys, Home and End by focusing and clicking the next choice; the buttons bind `[tabindex]` 0 for the chosen one and -1 for the rest (sign-in dialog: donor type and sign-in method; password reset: method).
